### Text4Seg++: Advancing Image Segmentation via Generative Language Modeling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.06321v1)
- **Authors**: Mengcheng Lan, Chaofeng Chen, Jiaxing Xu, Zongrui Li, Yiping Ke, Xudong Jiang, Yingchen Yu, Yunqing Zhao, Song Bai
- **Abstract**: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have shown exceptional capabilities in vision-language tasks. However, effectively integrating image segmentation into these models remains a significant challenge. In this work, we propose a novel text-as-mask paradigm that casts image segmentation as a text generation problem, eliminating the need for additional decoders and significantly simplifying the segmentation process. Our key innovation is semantic descriptors, a new textual representation of segmentation masks where each image patch is mapped to its corresponding text label. We first introduce image-wise semantic descriptors, a patch-aligned textual representation of segmentation masks that integrates naturally into the language modeling pipeline. To enhance efficiency, we introduce the Row-wise Run-Length Encoding (R-RLE), which compresses redundant text sequences, reducing the length of semantic descriptors by 74% and accelerating inference by $3\times$, without compromising performance. Building upon this, our initial framework Text4Seg achieves strong segmentation performance across a wide range of vision tasks. To further improve granularity and compactness, we propose box-wise semantic descriptors, which localizes regions of interest using bounding boxes and represents region masks via structured mask tokens called semantic bricks. This leads to our refined model, Text4Seg++, which formulates segmentation as a next-brick prediction task, combining precision, scalability, and generative efficiency. Comprehensive experiments on natural and remote sensing datasets show that Text4Seg++ consistently outperforms state-of-the-art models across diverse benchmarks without any task-specific fine-tuning, while remaining compatible with existing MLLM backbones. Our work highlights the effectiveness, scalability, and generalizability of text-driven image segmentation within the MLLM framework.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper "Text4Seg++: Advancing Image Segmentation via Generative Language Modeling":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel "text-as-mask" paradigm for image segmentation, integrating it directly into multimodal large language models (MLLMs). This paradigm reframes image segmentation as a text generation problem. Key innovations include:

1.  **Image-wise Semantic Descriptors (I-SD):**  Textual representations of segmentation masks where each image patch is mapped to a corresponding text label.
2.  **Row-wise Run-Length Encoding (R-RLE):**  A method to compress redundant text sequences in semantic descriptors, enhancing efficiency.
3.  **Box-wise Semantic Descriptors (B-SD):**  A more focused approach that localizes regions of interest using bounding boxes and represents region masks with structured mask tokens called semantic bricks.
4.  **Text4Seg++:** A framework based on the above features, that allows segmentation to be viewed as a next-brick prediction task.

The authors demonstrate that Text4Seg++ achieves state-of-the-art performance across diverse segmentation tasks without task-specific fine-tuning or architectural modifications to existing MLLMs. This approach leverages the generative capabilities of LLMs and streamlines the segmentation process.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a significant departure from traditional segmentation methods that rely on additional decoders or complex coordinate representations. The "text-as-mask" paradigm is a conceptually elegant approach. The semantic descriptors, particularly the box-wise version with semantic bricks, represent a novel way to encode segmentation information in a format that aligns well with LLMs. However, one could argue that discretizing the output space into semantic tokens is not entirely new. It's a clever application of well-known concepts to the domain of MLLMs and segmentation. The incremental improvement with Text4Seg++ showcases an iterative refinement, but doesn't introduce a completely new foundational idea beyond the core "text-as-mask" concept.
*   **Significance:** The work is significant because it addresses a key challenge in integrating image segmentation with MLLMs: the disparity between language and visual modalities. By framing segmentation as a text generation problem, the authors leverage the power of LLMs for segmentation tasks more effectively. The state-of-the-art results achieved by Text4Seg++ indicate a significant advancement in the field. It offers a more unified and scalable approach to segmentation compared to methods requiring task-specific decoders. Its ability to generalize across natural and remote sensing datasets is also valuable. The reduction in sequence length and improved efficiency due to R-RLE and semantic bricks contribute to its practical significance. The work also emphasizes the potential of generative models in dense prediction tasks, a promising avenue for future research.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Strong Performance:** Text4Seg++ consistently outperforms existing methods.
    *   **Unified Framework:** Avoids task-specific modifications, allowing for seamless integration with existing MLLMs.
    *   **Generative Approach:** Fully leverages the capabilities of LLMs.
    *   **Scalability and Efficiency:** R-RLE and semantic bricks improve efficiency without compromising performance.
    *   **Generalizability:** The approach works well across diverse datasets and tasks.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependency on Tokenization:** The reliance on tokenization can introduce artifacts, and the choice of tokenizer might influence performance.
    *   **Limited Architectural Modifications:** While avoiding extensive architectural changes is a strength, it also means that the model's performance might be bounded by the limitations of the underlying MLLM.
    *   **Computational Cost:** While efficient compared to alternatives, generating long text sequences can still be computationally intensive, especially for high-resolution images or very detailed segmentations.
    *   **Ablation results for Semantic Bricks:** The performance without semantic bricks wasn't significantly lower than with Semantic Bricks. More detail can be provided as to why, or how these can be improved for greater significance.
*   **Impact:** The paper is likely to have a significant impact on the field. It will influence future research on integrating segmentation with MLLMs and encourage the exploration of generative approaches for dense prediction tasks. The code and models released by the authors will facilitate further research and development.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:**

Text4Seg++ is a solid contribution and warrants a "8" score. The paper puts forward a conceptually interesting and effective approach. While the concept of discretizing the segmentation space isn't radically new, the authors present a well-engineered solution, demonstrating substantial performance gains across a variety of tasks without requiring specialized decoders or architectural changes to pre-existing MLLMs. The performance improvements and efficiency gains provide a solid foundation for subsequent work, and are significantly greater than other methods. The model is robust, efficient, and generalizable to different datasets. While the results of Semantic Bricks weren't very significant, they do allow for increased resolutions, and provides promise for future iterations. The core ideas of the model will open up new avenues for future research, making the paper have the potential to be an extremely valuable tool within the field of study.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Text-Trained LLMs Can Zero-Shot Extrapolate PDE Dynamics
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.06322v1)
- **Authors**: Jiajun Bao, Nicolas Boullé, Toni J. B. Liu, Raphaël Sarfati, Christopher J. Earls
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have demonstrated emergent in-context learning (ICL) capabilities across a range of tasks, including zero-shot time-series forecasting. We show that text-trained foundation models can accurately extrapolate spatiotemporal dynamics from discretized partial differential equation (PDE) solutions without fine-tuning or natural language prompting. Predictive accuracy improves with longer temporal contexts but degrades at finer spatial discretizations. In multi-step rollouts, where the model recursively predicts future spatial states over multiple time steps, errors grow algebraically with the time horizon, reminiscent of global error accumulation in classical finite-difference solvers. We interpret these trends as in-context neural scaling laws, where prediction quality varies predictably with both context length and output length. To better understand how LLMs are able to internally process PDE solutions so as to accurately roll them out, we analyze token-level output distributions and uncover a consistent ICL progression: beginning with syntactic pattern imitation, transitioning through an exploratory high-entropy phase, and culminating in confident, numerically grounded predictions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the ability of text-trained Large Language Models (LLMs) to extrapolate the dynamics of partial differential equations (PDEs) directly from serialized solution data in a zero-shot, in-context learning (ICL) setting.  The authors demonstrate that LLMs can accurately continue spatiotemporal dynamics of PDEs without fine-tuning or natural language prompts. They analyze the performance with respect to context length, spatial discretization, and rollout horizon, and reveal behaviors analogous to truncation errors in classical numerical analysis.  Furthermore, the paper analyzes token-level predictive entropy and reveals a three-stage progression in ICL behavior during spatiotemporal PDE continuation: syntactic pattern imitation, exploratory high-entropy phase, and confident numerical grounded predictions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**

    * **Interesting Application of LLMs:**  The work explores a novel and intriguing application of LLMs, going beyond typical language-based tasks to examine numerical reasoning capabilities in the context of PDE solutions. This is a valuable contribution to understanding the emergent abilities of LLMs.
    * **Rigorous Experimental Setup:** The paper employs a controlled experimental setup, systematically varying key parameters like context length and spatial discretization, and comparing against classical numerical methods to provide a solid benchmark. This allows for a quantitative assessment of LLM performance.
    * **Insightful Analysis:** The analysis of in-context scaling laws and the three-stage ICL progression provides valuable insights into *how* LLMs process and extrapolate PDE dynamics. The uncertainty analysis is particularly insightful.
    * **Code Availability:**  The provision of code promotes reproducibility and allows others to build upon their findings.
    * **Generalization:** Experiments across a range of PDEs (Allen-Cahn, Fisher-KPP, Heat, Wave) increases the impact and validity of the findings.

* **Weaknesses:**

    * **Limited Novelty in Terms of LLM Architecture:** The paper focuses on *using* existing pre-trained LLMs rather than proposing any new LLM architecture tailored for PDE solving. This makes it an application-focused study rather than one pushing the boundaries of LLM architectures themselves.
    * **PDE Solution Representation:** While using a serialized sequence of numbers avoids natural language, it could be argued that this representation *implicitly* provides some structure that aids the LLM, even without explicit prompting. It is unclear how LLMs process the PDE solutions under serialization which has been observed to be crucial for performance.
    * **Lack of Theoretical Justification:** The paper lacks a strong theoretical framework to explain the observed scaling laws and the three-stage ICL progression. It provides empirical observations but does not offer a deep theoretical explanation of *why* these behaviors emerge. The authors model ICL as Markov-Chain but do not explain how it can be used in practical applications.
    * **Practical Applicability:**  While the work is interesting, it's not immediately clear how these findings could lead to practical improvements in PDE solvers. The LLMs are not outperforming existing numerical methods and the computational costs are not discussed. The claim of ICL helping conservation is not practically useful as any finite-difference scheme can be constructed to help with conservation laws.

* **Novelty and Significance:**

    * **Novelty:** The specific combination of zero-shot PDE extrapolation using LLMs, combined with the detailed scaling and entropy analysis, represents a novel contribution.  It builds on existing work in time-series forecasting with LLMs but extends it to the more complex domain of spatiotemporal PDE dynamics.
    * **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its potential to unlock a deeper understanding of the numerical reasoning capabilities embedded within large language models. It provides a lens for analyzing the inductive biases and numerical priors that emerge during pre-training. Even though it is not a practical PDE solver, this better understanding could potentially inform the design of new architectures or training strategies that *do* lead to improvements in scientific computing.

* **Potential Influence:**

    * The paper will likely stimulate further research into the numerical reasoning abilities of LLMs. It provides a benchmark and methodology for evaluating these capabilities in the context of PDE solutions. The observed scaling laws and ICL progression can serve as starting points for theoretical investigations.

**Score:**

Score: 6.5

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel application of LLMs and provides some insightful empirical observations and analysis. However, it falls short of a truly high score due to the limited novelty in terms of LLM architecture, lack of deep theoretical explanation, and unclear immediate practical impact on PDE solving. It is not immediately clear how the findings can be used in applications. While the findings are interesting, the practical use is limited as it is computationally expensive to use LLMs and not very accurate and thus not very important in PDE research, or LLM research. It demonstrates an ability of LLMs to learn but does not provide a framework or analysis that is very unique. The contribution is incremental, adding another domain where LLMs exhibits ICL but without a breakthrough discovery.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### AttestLLM: Efficient Attestation Framework for Billion-scale On-device LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.06326v1)
- **Authors**: Ruisi Zhang, Yifei Zhao, Neusha Javidnia, Mengxin Zheng, Farinaz Koushanfar
- **Abstract**: As on-device LLMs(e.g., Apple on-device Intelligence) are widely adopted to reduce network dependency, improve privacy, and enhance responsiveness, verifying the legitimacy of models running on local devices becomes critical. Existing attestation techniques are not suitable for billion-parameter Large Language Models (LLMs), struggling to remain both time- and memory-efficient while addressing emerging threats in the LLM era. In this paper, we present AttestLLM, the first-of-its-kind attestation framework to protect the hardware-level intellectual property (IP) of device vendors by ensuring that only authorized LLMs can execute on target platforms. AttestLLM leverages an algorithm/software/hardware co-design approach to embed robust watermarking signatures onto the activation distributions of LLM building blocks. It also optimizes the attestation protocol within the Trusted Execution Environment (TEE), providing efficient verification without compromising inference throughput. Extensive proof-of-concept evaluations on LLMs from Llama, Qwen, and Phi families for on-device use cases demonstrate AttestLLM's attestation reliability, fidelity, and efficiency. Furthermore, AttestLLM enforces model legitimacy and exhibits resilience against model replacement and forgery attacks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AttestLLM, a novel attestation framework designed to protect the hardware-level intellectual property (IP) of device vendors in the context of on-device Large Language Models (LLMs).  As on-device LLMs become more prevalent, the framework addresses the challenge of verifying the legitimacy of models running locally. AttestLLM achieves this through a combination of offline watermarking and online attestation using Trusted Execution Environments (TEEs).  The offline watermarking process embeds robust signatures into the activation distributions of LLM building blocks using a sensitivity-aware optimization approach. The online attestation, performed within a TEE, efficiently verifies these watermarks without significantly impacting inference throughput.  The paper presents experimental results on various LLM families (Llama, Qwen, Phi), demonstrating AttestLLM's effectiveness in terms of attestation reliability, model fidelity, and efficiency, while also showcasing its resilience against model replacement and forgery attacks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its development of an attestation framework specifically tailored for billion-scale LLMs running on resource-constrained edge devices. While watermarking and attestation techniques exist separately, their combination and optimization for this specific scenario represents a significant contribution. The co-design of the watermarking algorithm with hardware platform constraints is crucial.

*   **Significance:** The work addresses a practical and increasingly important security challenge. As hardware vendors integrate LLMs directly into edge devices, ensuring the integrity and authorization of these models becomes critical for protecting their IP and maintaining user trust. The ability to prevent unauthorized model execution has significant implications for both the security and business models associated with on-device LLMs. It strengthens the trustworthiness of edge AI deployments.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of hardware IP infringement related to on-device LLMs.
    *   **Comprehensive Solution:**  AttestLLM provides a holistic approach by integrating watermarking, TEE-based attestation, and system-level optimizations.
    *   **Hardware-Software Co-Design:** The framework acknowledges the constraints of edge devices and designs the solution accordingly (virtualization based TEE, dynamic sampling). This is a crucial aspect for practical adoption.
    *   **Extensive Evaluation:** The evaluation encompasses various LLM architectures, quantization levels, and attack scenarios, providing strong evidence for the framework's effectiveness. It also compares performance against the naive "TEE Shield" approach.
    *   **Practical Considerations:** The paper discusses practical implementation details, such as dynamic attestation and the ability to trade-off between security and overhead. It mentions that the attestation API can be integrated with real-world LLM deployments.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on TEEs:** The security of AttestLLM heavily relies on the security of the underlying TEE (Arm TrustZone). While the framework mitigates some of TrustZone's limitations, vulnerabilities in the TEE itself could compromise the entire system.
    *   **Potential Scalability Issues:** The framework does not provide results on much larger LLMs. The current results show testing up to 15B parameter LLMs. It's worth investigating whether current implementation can efficiently work with hundreds of billions of parameters or trillion parameter LLMs.
    *   **Limited Analysis of Adversarial Capabilities:** While the paper addresses model replacement and forgery attacks, a deeper analysis of more sophisticated adversarial strategies would strengthen the evaluation. For instance, how easy is it to bypass the attestation if memory encryption is broken?

*   **Impact:** AttestLLM has the potential to significantly influence the design of secure on-device AI systems. The framework provides a foundation for ensuring the trustworthiness of LLMs deployed on edge devices, which could accelerate the adoption of privacy-preserving and computationally efficient AI applications.
*   **Missing pieces**
    *   **Detailed implementation specs:** Lack the details for low-level implementations, such as memory access patterns and power consumption overhead.
    *   **Ethical considerations:** While protecting IP is important, discussion about the ethical implications of controlling models running on local devices could enhance the paper.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the paper's novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, a score of **7.5** is justified. The paper makes a strong contribution by developing a practical and well-evaluated attestation framework for on-device LLMs. The co-design approach, comprehensive evaluation, and consideration of real-world constraints are commendable. However, the reliance on TEE security and the need for further scalability and robustness analysis prevent a higher score. There is still much that can be done with this direction, namely, evaluating with larger models, a discussion of ethical aspects of protecting IP, and details about low-level implementation details.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### A Fragile Number Sense: Probing the Elemental Limits of Numerical Reasoning in LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.06332v1)
- **Authors**: Roussel Rahman, Aashwin Ananda Mishra
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated remarkable emergent capabilities, yet the robustness of their numerical reasoning remains an open question. While standard benchmarks evaluate LLM reasoning on complex problem sets using aggregated metrics, they often obscure foundational weaknesses. In this work, we probe LLM mathematical numeracy by evaluating performance on problems of escalating complexity, from constituent operations to combinatorial puzzles. We test several state-of-the-art LLM-based agents on a 100-problem challenge comprising four categories: (1) basic arithmetic, (2) advanced operations, (3) primality checking, and (4) the Game of 24 number puzzle. Our results show that while the agents achieved high accuracy on the first three categories, which require deterministic algorithmic execution, they consistently failed at the number puzzle, underlining its demand for a heuristic search over a large combinatorial space to be a significant bottleneck. These findings reveal that the agents' proficiency is largely confined to recalling and executing known algorithms, rather than performing generative problem-solving. This suggests their apparent numerical reasoning is more akin to sophisticated pattern-matching than flexible, analytical thought, limiting their potential for tasks that require novel or creative numerical insights.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "A Fragile Number Sense: Probing the Elemental Limits of Numerical Reasoning in LLMs" investigates the numerical reasoning capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) by evaluating their performance on a hierarchical set of mathematical problems. The challenges range from basic arithmetic to more complex tasks like primality checking and the "Game of 24." The core finding is that while LLMs perform reasonably well on tasks requiring deterministic algorithms, their performance significantly drops on the "Game of 24," which demands a heuristic search within a large combinatorial space.  This suggests that LLMs primarily rely on pattern matching and recall of learned algorithms rather than flexible analytical problem-solving, thus demonstrating a fragility in their numerical "number sense."

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's contribution lies in its targeted approach to dissecting LLM numerical reasoning.  While the limitations of LLMs in mathematical tasks have been noted before, this paper goes beyond aggregated benchmark scores to specifically identify *where* the reasoning breaks down.  The use of the Game of 24 as a diagnostic tool is a clever choice, as it isolates the need for heuristic search from complex mathematical operations. However, the lack of mechanistic interpretability analysis represents a missed opportunity. Identifying specific circuits or patterns in the LLM's internal representation that contribute to the observed fragility would have significantly strengthened the paper's contribution.

*   **Significance:** The findings are significant because they highlight a crucial limitation in LLM reasoning. The reliance on pattern matching over true analytical thought could have implications for the use of LLMs in applications that require novel numerical insights or creative problem-solving.  The divide-and-reconstruct methodology for analyzing LLM performance offers a valuable framework for future research. The implications of these findings may include the use of human-computer symbiosis for solving complex mathematical problems and limitations in automated decision making.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The experimental design is well-structured, with a clear progression in problem complexity.
    *   The choice of the Game of 24 is a particularly effective way to isolate the heuristic search component of numerical reasoning.
    *   The analysis of error types provides valuable insights into the nature of LLM failures.
    *   The "divide-and-reconstruct" methodology is a promising framework for analyzing LLM capabilities.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The paper doesn't delve into *why* LLMs struggle with the Game of 24 beyond the assertion that it requires heuristic search. What specific aspects of the task make it difficult?
    *   There's a lack of connection to the wider literature on human mathematical cognition and problem-solving (beyond the brief mention of number sense). How does the LLM's approach compare to human strategies for solving these problems?
    *   The paper would be strengthened by exploring different prompting strategies or fine-tuning approaches to mitigate the observed limitations.
    *   The paper does not perform an ablation study on components of reasoning such as number sense vs memory.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper is likely to influence future research in LLM evaluation and design. It provides a concrete example of a task that exposes a fundamental limitation in LLM reasoning and offers a useful methodology for analyzing LLM capabilities. The paper underscores the need to develop LLMs that can move beyond pattern matching and engage in more analytical problem-solving.

**Score: 6**

**Rationale:**

While the paper makes a valuable contribution by highlighting a key limitation of LLMs in numerical reasoning, its novelty and significance are somewhat limited by the lack of a deeper exploration of *why* this limitation exists and how it could be addressed. The paper presents a well-executed experimental study, but it lacks the theoretical depth or mechanistic interpretability that would elevate it to a higher score. Also, the paper is a replication of prior work that discovered the limitations of LLMs in solving mathematical problems.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### Harnessing Object Grounding for Time-Sensitive Video Understanding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.06335v1)
- **Authors**: Tz-Ying Wu, Sharath Nittur Sridhar, Subarna Tripathi
- **Abstract**: We propose to improve the time-sensitive video understanding (TSV) capability of video large language models (Video-LLMs) with grounded objects (GO). We hypothesize that TSV tasks can benefit from GO within frames, which is supported by our preliminary experiments on LITA, a state-of-the-art Video-LLM for reasoning temporal localization. While augmenting prompts with textual description of these object annotations improves the performance of LITA, it also introduces extra token length and susceptibility to the noise in object level information. To address this, we propose GO-Tokenizer, a lightweight add-on module for Video-LLMs leveraging off-the-shelf object detectors to encode compact object information on the fly. Experimental results demonstrate that pretraining with GO-Tokenizer outperforms the vanilla Video-LLM and its counterpart utilizing textual description of objects in the prompt. The gain generalizes across different models, datasets and video understanding tasks such as reasoning temporal localization and dense captioning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Harnessing Object Grounding for Time-Sensitive Video Understanding" proposes a novel approach to enhance time-sensitive video understanding (TSV) in video large language models (Video-LLMs). The core idea is to incorporate grounded object (GO) information – specifically, the semantics, spatial positions, and temporal cues of objects within video frames – to improve the model's ability to reason about events and actions that unfold over time.  The authors introduce a "GO-Tokenizer," a lightweight module that encodes these object attributes into compact object tokens, mitigating the token length limitations associated with LLMs. They demonstrate that pretraining with the GO-Tokenizer outperforms vanilla Video-LLMs and those augmented with textual object descriptions across various tasks like reasoning temporal localization and dense captioning. The gains are shown to be robust across different models (LITA and TimeChat) and datasets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The concept of integrating object grounding for TSV in Video-LLMs is not entirely new, but the authors propose a concrete and efficient way to do so using a dedicated GO-Tokenizer. The GO-Tokenizer, specifically the use of ROI patch pooling combined with time-aware positional encoding to create compact object tokens, is a valuable contribution. This addresses the key limitation of existing methods which either lack object-level information or rely on lengthy, token-inefficient textual descriptions.

*   **Significance:** The paper tackles a crucial challenge in Video-LLMs: balancing temporal and spatial resolution under token length constraints. TSV is a fundamental aspect of video understanding, and the ability to ground events in specific objects is essential for many applications. The proposed GO-Tokenizer provides a practical solution that significantly improves performance on TSV tasks. The empirical results, demonstrating consistent improvements across different models and datasets, further reinforces the significance.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined problem:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing Video-LLMs for TSV due to token constraints.
    *   **Effective solution:** The GO-Tokenizer is a well-designed module that efficiently encodes object information. ROI patch pooling and time-aware positional embeddings seem to be key to the module's success.
    *   **Strong empirical evaluation:** The experiments are comprehensive, including comparisons to vanilla Video-LLMs, those augmented with textual descriptions, and ablation studies to understand the contribution of different components. The evaluations are conducted on two different models and datasets to demonstrate generalization.
    *   **Practicality:** The approach is practical in that it leverages off-the-shelf object detectors and can be easily integrated into existing Video-LLM architectures.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Object Detectors:** The performance of the GO-Tokenizer is highly dependent on the quality of the object detector used. While the authors mitigate this somewhat through robust feature extraction (ROI pooling), the inherent limitations of object detectors in complex scenes (e.g., occlusions, unusual viewpoints) could still impact performance. The dependence needs more critical discussion.
    *   **Limited Scope of Objects:** The method focuses primarily on the presence and location of objects, potentially neglecting other aspects of object-level understanding (e.g., attributes, relationships between objects).  The paper would be stronger with a deeper exploration of this limitation.
    *   **Qualitative Results:** The qualitative results are limited.  Including more diverse examples and a discussion of failure cases would be valuable.

*   **Impact:** The paper introduces a practical and effective method for incorporating object-level information into Video-LLMs for TSV.  The GO-Tokenizer's efficiency and generalizability make it a valuable contribution that could influence the design of future Video-LLMs. It will likely spur further research in object-aware video understanding.
    *   **Originality concerns:** while the GO tokenizer is a strong contribution, the use of bounding box embeddings in the video setting have been seen before in other papers, as well as the fact that having object grounding improves reasoning. The novelty is thus more so the novel application of combining these elements in a specific form.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**
The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of Video-LLMs by addressing a key challenge in time-sensitive video understanding. The GO-Tokenizer is a well-designed and practical solution that effectively incorporates object-level information while mitigating token length limitations. The strong empirical results, demonstrating consistent improvements across different models and datasets, further reinforce the significance. However, the reliance on object detectors and a somewhat limited scope of object-level understanding, along with the lack of more critical discussion regarding the originality of the problem, detracts from the overall impact. This score reflects the novelty and significance of the work, while acknowledging its limitations and areas for improvement.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Large Language Models as Virtual Survey Respondents: Evaluating Sociodemographic Response Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.06337v1)
- **Authors**: Jianpeng Zhao, Chenyu Yuan, Weiming Luo, Haoling Xie, Guangwei Zhang, Steven Jige Quan, Zixuan Yuan, Pengyang Wang, Denghui Zhang
- **Abstract**: Questionnaire-based surveys are foundational to social science research and public policymaking, yet traditional survey methods remain costly, time-consuming, and often limited in scale. This paper explores a new paradigm: simulating virtual survey respondents using Large Language Models (LLMs). We introduce two novel simulation settings, namely Partial Attribute Simulation (PAS) and Full Attribute Simulation (FAS), to systematically evaluate the ability of LLMs to generate accurate and demographically coherent responses. In PAS, the model predicts missing attributes based on partial respondent profiles, whereas FAS involves generating complete synthetic datasets under both zero-context and context-enhanced conditions. We curate a comprehensive benchmark suite, LLM-S^3 (Large Language Model-based Sociodemographic Survey Simulation), that spans 11 real-world public datasets across four sociological domains. Our evaluation of multiple mainstream LLMs (GPT-3.5/4 Turbo, LLaMA 3.0/3.1-8B) reveals consistent trends in prediction performance, highlights failure modes, and demonstrates how context and prompt design impact simulation fidelity. This work establishes a rigorous foundation for LLM-driven survey simulations, offering scalable and cost-effective tools for sociological research and policy evaluation. Our code and dataset are available at: https://github.com/dart-lab-research/LLM-S-Cube-Benchmark
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

This paper introduces a new paradigm for simulating virtual survey respondents using Large Language Models (LLMs). The authors propose two novel simulation settings: Partial Attribute Simulation (PAS), where LLMs predict missing respondent attributes based on partial profiles, and Full Attribute Simulation (FAS), where LLMs generate complete synthetic datasets under zero-context and context-enhanced conditions. They curate LLM-S³, a comprehensive benchmark with 11 real-world public datasets across four sociological domains, to evaluate LLMs (GPT-3.5/4 Turbo, LLaMA 3.0/3.1-8B). The evaluation reveals consistent performance trends, highlights failure modes, and demonstrates the impact of context and prompt design on simulation fidelity. The study establishes a foundation for LLM-driven survey simulations, offering scalable and cost-effective tools for sociological research and policy evaluation.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novelty:** The paper's core contribution lies in framing LLMs as virtual survey respondents and formalizing this concept through PAS and FAS. While prior work has explored LLMs in social simulation, this paper offers a structured methodology and extensive benchmark for systematic evaluation.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** LLM-S³ provides a valuable resource for assessing LLMs' capabilities in generating sociodemographic responses. The use of 11 real-world datasets across different sociological domains enhances the generalizability of the findings. The inclusion of zero-shot and few-shot evaluations adds further rigor.
    *   **Practical Relevance:**  The paper addresses a real-world need in social science research: the high cost and limitations of traditional survey methods. LLM-driven survey simulations offer a potentially scalable and cost-effective alternative.
    *   **Actionable Insights:** The paper not only presents experimental results but also provides practical design principles for future research and deployment, such as the importance of prompt design and contextual augmentation.
    *   **Reproducibility**: the work provides code and dataset available at a public repository.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Failure Mode Analysis:** While the paper mentions failure modes (e.g., structured output generation in FAS), a more in-depth analysis of the reasons for these failures and potential mitigation strategies would strengthen the work.
    *   **LLM Diversity:** While the paper evaluates GPT and LLaMA models, including a wider range of LLMs with varying architectures and training data could offer a more comprehensive picture of the landscape.
    *   **Statistical Validation:** More robust statistical validation of the synthetic data against the original datasets could strengthen the claim that LLMs can generate representative populations.  Beyond KL divergence, exploring other statistical measures of similarity (e.g., propensity score weighting, causal inference frameworks for evaluating synthetic data) would enhance rigor.
    *   **Bias Mitigation Details**: While the in-context learning bias mitigation strategy is discussed, more detail on the strict example balancing measures would be valuable for reproducibility.
    *   **Ethical Considerations:**  A more thorough discussion of the ethical implications of using LLMs for survey simulation is necessary. Concerns about potential biases, privacy, and the responsible use of synthetic data should be addressed.
    *   **Domain-Specific Nuances**:  While the multi-domain benchmark is a strength, it also leads to a broad, less-deep analysis of each specific sociological domain. Future work could focus on optimizing LLM simulations for particular research areas.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper opens up a new avenue for social science research by demonstrating the potential of LLMs to simulate survey respondents.
    *   The LLM-S³ benchmark will likely become a valuable tool for evaluating and comparing LLMs in this context.
    *   The findings contribute to a better understanding of LLMs' capabilities and limitations in generating sociodemographic responses.
    *   The proposed simulation methodologies could have a significant impact on policy formulation and social science research by enabling more scalable and cost-effective data collection and analysis.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper represents a valuable contribution to the emerging field of LLM-driven social simulation. It offers a novel methodology, a comprehensive benchmark, and actionable insights. While there are areas for improvement, the paper's strengths outweigh its weaknesses, and its potential impact on social science research is significant. The methodology provides a solid foundation for future investigations. Given these factors, and the existing limitations within the field related to rigorous, systematic approaches to LLM social simulation, I believe a score of 7 is warranted.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Embedding Poisoning: Bypassing Safety Alignment via Embedding Semantic Shift
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.06338v1)
- **Authors**: Shuai Yuan, Zhibo Zhang, Yuxi Li, Guangdong Bai, Wang Kailong
- **Abstract**: The widespread distribution of Large Language Models (LLMs) through public platforms like Hugging Face introduces significant security challenges. While these platforms perform basic security scans, they often fail to detect subtle manipulations within the embedding layer. This work identifies a novel class of deployment phase attacks that exploit this vulnerability by injecting imperceptible perturbations directly into the embedding layer outputs without modifying model weights or input text. These perturbations, though statistically benign, systematically bypass safety alignment mechanisms and induce harmful behaviors during inference. We propose Search based Embedding Poisoning(SEP), a practical, model agnostic framework that introduces carefully optimized perturbations into embeddings associated with high risk tokens. SEP leverages a predictable linear transition in model responses, from refusal to harmful output to semantic deviation to identify a narrow perturbation window that evades alignment safeguards. Evaluated across six aligned LLMs, SEP achieves an average attack success rate of 96.43% while preserving benign task performance and evading conventional detection mechanisms. Our findings reveal a critical oversight in deployment security and emphasize the urgent need for embedding level integrity checks in future LLM defense strategies.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel attack strategy called Search-based Embedding Poisoning (SEP) that targets Large Language Models (LLMs) by injecting subtle, carefully crafted perturbations into the embedding layer outputs. This attack bypasses traditional safety alignment mechanisms (SFT, RLHF) that rely on accurate semantic encoding within embeddings.  SEP identifies high-risk tokens, and uses a search strategy to find minimal perturbations that steer the model away from refusal responses and toward generating harmful content, without significantly impacting performance on benign tasks. The attack is model-agnostic, meaning it can be applied to different LLMs. The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of SEP across several aligned LLMs, achieving high attack success rates while evading conventional detection methods. The work highlights a critical security oversight in current LLM deployment security: the lack of embedding-level integrity checks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the following aspects:
    *   **Targeting the Embedding Layer (Deployment-Phase):** While adversarial attacks against LLMs are not new, the focus on manipulating the embedding layer *after* model deployment is a relatively unexplored attack vector.  Existing work primarily focuses on prompt engineering or model poisoning during training or fine-tuning.  This deployment phase attack is distinct.
    *   **Semantic Shift via Controlled Perturbation:** The discovery of a predictable, linear transition in model responses (from refusal to harmful output to semantic deviation) through controlled embedding perturbation is a key novel finding. This predictability allows for the efficient search strategy employed by SEP.
    *   **Practical and Model-Agnostic Framework:** The creation of a practical, easily implementable, and model-agnostic attack framework contributes to the novelty of the approach.

* **Significance:** The paper carries significant implications for LLM security and deployment:
    *   **Undermining Safety Alignment:** The finding that safety alignment mechanisms are vulnerable to subtle embedding perturbations is a serious concern. It suggests that current safety measures, primarily focused on parameter adjustments and text-based input filtering, are insufficient.
    *   **Highlighting a Critical Security Gap:** The paper exposes a significant gap in LLM deployment security. Model distribution platforms like Hugging Face typically perform security scans, but those scans might not detect subtle manipulations within the embedding layer that can have drastic consequences.
    *   **Implications for Trust and Security:**  The findings underscore the need for more robust security protocols, including embedding-level integrity checks and adversarial robustness measures, to ensure the safe and reliable deployment of LLMs.  The work raises important questions about the trustworthiness of LLMs distributed through public platforms.

* **Strengths:**
    *   **Well-Designed Empirical Study:** The empirical analysis is thorough and well-executed. The controlled experiments, comprehensive benchmark, and comparison with existing methods provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of SEP.
    *   **Clear and Concise Writing:** The paper is well-written and easy to understand. The concepts are explained clearly, and the experimental results are presented in a compelling manner.
    *   **Reproducibility:**  The inclusion of a demonstration link contributes to the reproducibility of the work.

* **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Computational Cost:** While the authors claim sublinear time complexity, the experimental results show a significant variance in Q/TC (queries per test case) for different models. The efficiency of SEP might be a concern for very large models or in real-time applications. While they utilize sampling to get the dimensions it's still unclear which factors dictate this computational cost.
    *   **Limited Scope of Defenses Evaluated:** While the paper compares against some existing defenses, a more extensive evaluation against a wider range of defensive mechanisms would strengthen the conclusions.
    *   **Reliance on External Classifiers:** The attack relies on external classifiers to evaluate the semantic meaning and harmfulness of the output. The performance of SEP is inherently linked to the quality of these classifiers. While the authors have shown the classifiers to have high accuracy, the generalizability of SEP will be determined by the applicability of said classifiers.
    *   **Potential for Future Mitigation:** While the paper presents strong evidence of the vulnerability, it's possible that future safety alignment strategies could incorporate embedding-level protections that could mitigate SEP. This suggests further refinement of the current solution to circumvent newer defenses.

* **Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **8**.  The paper presents a novel attack vector with significant implications for LLM security. The experimental results are strong, and the findings are well-supported. The work highlights a critical security gap that needs to be addressed by the research community and industry.  The primary reason for not assigning a higher score is the dependence on external classifiers and the potential for future mitigation. The computational cost and the scope of evaluated defenses could also be improved.  However, the work is undoubtedly a significant contribution to the field and will likely stimulate further research on LLM security.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 8/10

### Evaluating Multi-Turn Bargain Skills in LLM-Based Seller Agent
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.06341v1)
- **Authors**: Issue Yishu Wang, Kakam Chong, Xiaofeng Wang, Xu Yan, DeXin Kong, Chen Ju, Ming Chen, Shuai Xiao, Shuguang Han, jufeng chen
- **Abstract**: In online second-hand marketplaces, multi-turn bargaining is a crucial part of seller-buyer interactions. Large Language Models (LLMs) can act as seller agents, negotiating with buyers on behalf of sellers under given business constraints. A critical ability for such agents is to track and accurately interpret cumulative buyer intents across long negotiations, which directly impacts bargaining effectiveness. We introduce a multi-turn evaluation framework for measuring the bargaining ability of seller agents in e-commerce dialogues. The framework tests whether an agent can extract and track buyer intents. Our contributions are: (1) a large-scale e-commerce bargaining benchmark spanning 622 categories, 9,892 products, and 3,014 tasks; (2) a turn-level evaluation framework grounded in Theory of Mind (ToM) with annotated buyer intents, moving beyond outcome-only metrics; and (3) an automated pipeline that extracts reliable intent from massive dialogue data.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Evaluating Multi-Turn Bargain Skills in LLM-Based Seller Agents":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a framework, BargainBench, to evaluate the bargaining abilities of LLM-based seller agents in e-commerce dialogues. It focuses on the agent's ability to track and understand buyer intents across multiple turns in a negotiation.  The framework includes a large-scale benchmark dataset extracted from e-commerce interactions, a turn-level evaluation approach grounded in Theory of Mind (ToM) using annotated buyer intents, and an automated intent extraction pipeline. The authors tested various LLMs using their benchmark and evaluated performance based on intent recognition, precision, recall, and failure rates.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper offers moderate novelty.  The idea of using LLMs as seller agents and evaluating their bargaining skills is interesting and timely, given the increasing use of LLMs in various applications. While other benchmarks exist for negotiation and dialogue, the focus on *turn-level intent tracking* in an e-commerce setting is a valuable contribution. The grounding in ToM is a thoughtful touch, but the depth of ToM implementation could be explored further. The intent-action-tool hierarchy adds a layer of granularity that is potentially useful for diagnosing model performance.

*   **Significance:** The work has potential significance for the development of more effective and reliable e-commerce agents.  By focusing on intent understanding, the benchmark encourages models to move beyond shallow pattern matching.  The introduction of failure rate as a metric is particularly relevant for real-world applications, as it highlights the model's reliability and safety. The automated pipeline for generating labeled data is crucial for scaling and reproducibility. The evaluation and detailed analysis of the LLM's performance on various metrics in different conditions/categories is good.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Dataset:** The large-scale, real-world e-commerce dataset is a significant asset, providing a realistic and challenging environment for evaluating bargaining skills. The size and diversity are a key strength compared to previous benchmarks.
    *   **Evaluation Framework:** The turn-level evaluation, with ground truth buyer intents, allows for a more nuanced assessment of model performance compared to outcome-only metrics.
    *   **Intent Hierarchy:** The decomposition of negotiation behavior into the intent-action-tool hierarchy offers a structured approach to understanding and improving model performance.
    *   **Automated Pipeline:** The automated pipeline for intent extraction is a practical contribution, enabling scalable and reproducible benchmarking.
    *   **Failure Rate:** The metric to evaluate stability of different LLMs is important as it highlights models' reliability in real-world applications.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Task Simplification:** While focusing on intent tracking is valuable, there is potential oversimplification. Real-world bargaining involves more complex factors like emotions, social dynamics, and external knowledge, which are not fully captured in this framework.
    *   **LLM for Dialogue Generation:** The Problem Weaver utilizes an LLM to generate dialogues. While useful for scaling, this introduces potential bias and limits the diversity of interactions.  A more controlled or diverse set of scripted dialogues might be beneficial.
    *   **ToM Implementation:** Although the mention of ToM as an idea is good, the exactness or sophistication of its implementation could be more deeply explored. For example, the paper focuses on *recognizing* the intent. A better test of ToM would be to have the agent *reason about* the intent to make strategic decisions (more than just whether to accept or reject).

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence the development of better LLM-based e-commerce agents. The benchmark can be used by other researchers to evaluate their models, and the insights from the analysis of LLM performance can guide future research directions.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the above analysis, I assign a score of **7**. The paper is valuable due to its novel focus on turn-level intent tracking in e-commerce bargaining, the large-scale dataset, and the automated intent extraction pipeline. However, the simplifications in the task and the potential limitations of relying on LLMs for dialogue generation, as well as shallow ToM application, weaken its overall impact. While it's a good step forward, further research is needed to address these limitations and create truly sophisticated bargaining agents.
